- [ ] **Step 2: The Jev eval**

`eval/run.ts`:

```ts
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { interpretationQuestions, interpretationState, judgmentClientFromEnv, type InterpretationAnswers } from "@cheiron/judgment";

interface Case { q: string; intent?: string[]; dimension?: string[]; secondary_dimension?: string[]; edge_kind?: string[]; metric?: string[]; chart?: string[]; flags?: Record<string, boolean>; entities?: Record<string, unknown> }
const cases = JSON.parse(readFileSync(new URL("./queries.json", import.meta.url), "utf8")) as Case[];
const judge = judgmentClientFromEnv();
if (!judge.enabled) { console.error("TYPESAFE_API_KEY is not set"); process.exit(1); }

const rows: string[] = []; const latencies: number[] = []; let hits = 0, checks = 0; const confusions: string[] = [];
for (const c of cases) {
  const r = await judge.ask({ state: interpretationState(c.q, {}), questions: interpretationQuestions() });
  if (!r.ok) { rows.push(`| ${c.q} | FAILED ${r.reason} | | | |`); continue; }
  const a = r.answers as InterpretationAnswers; latencies.push(r.ms);
  const cell = (key: keyof Case, got: string, p: number) => { const exp = c[key] as string[] | undefined; if (!exp) return `${got} (${p.toFixed(2)})`; checks++; const ok = exp.includes(got); if (ok) hits++; else confusions.push(`${c.q}: ${String(key)} expected ${exp.join("|")}, got ${got} (${p.toFixed(2)})`); return `${ok ? "✓" : "✗"} ${got} (${p.toFixed(2)})`; };
  const flagCell = Object.entries(c.flags ?? {}).map(([k, v]) => { const n = (a[k as keyof InterpretationAnswers] as { noul: number }).noul; checks++; const ok = v ? n >= 0.5 : n < 0.5; if (ok) hits++; else confusions.push(`${c.q}: ${k} expected ${v}, got ${n.toFixed(2)}`); return `${ok ? "✓" : "✗"} ${k}=${n.toFixed(2)}`; }).join(" ");
  rows.push(`| ${c.q} | ${cell("intent", a.intent.choice, a.intent.probabilities[a.intent.choice] ?? 0)} | ${cell("dimension", a.dimension.choice, a.dimension.probabilities[a.dimension.choice] ?? 0)} | ${cell("chart", a.chart.choice, a.chart.probabilities[a.chart.choice] ?? 0)} | ${flagCell} | ${r.ms} |`);
}
latencies.sort((x, y) => x - y);
const pct = (p: number) => latencies[Math.min(latencies.length - 1, Math.floor(p * latencies.length))] ?? 0;
const report = [
  `# Jev agreement on ${cases.length} labelled questions`, "", `Run: ${new Date().toISOString()} · model: ${judge.model} · agreement: ${hits}/${checks} (${Math.round((100 * hits) / Math.max(1, checks))}%) · latency p50 ${pct(0.5)} ms, p90 ${pct(0.9)} ms`, "",
  "| Question | Intent | Dimension | Chart | Flags | ms |", "|---|---|---|---|---|---|", ...rows, "",
  "## Disagreements", "", ...(confusions.length ? confusions.map((x) => `- ${x}`) : ["None."]), "",
].join("\n");
writeFileSync(new URL("./report.md", import.meta.url), report);
console.log(report);
```

Run: `npm run eval:jev`. Expected: a table, agreement above 85%. For every disagreement, decide: is the label wrong (fix `queries.json`), or is the criterion text unclear (fix the wording in `packages/judgment/src/interpretation.ts`, bump nothing yet since version 1 has not shipped)? Re-run until the remaining disagreements are ones you can defend in the README. Do not loosen the labels to make the number look good.

- [ ] **Step 3: The extractor benchmark**

`eval/extract-bench.ts`:

```ts
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { openAiExtractor } from "@cheiron/engine";

interface Case { q: string; entities?: { drugs?: string[]; conditions?: string[]; sponsors?: string[]; countries?: string[]; start_year?: number; end_year?: number; limit?: number } }
const cases = (JSON.parse(readFileSync(new URL("./queries.json", import.meta.url), "utf8")) as Case[]).filter((c) => c.entities);
const models = (process.env.BENCH_MODELS ?? "gpt-5.4-nano,gpt-4.1-nano").split(",");
const norm = (xs: string[] = []) => xs.map((x) => x.toLowerCase().trim()).sort();
const overlaps = (got: string[], exp: string[]) => exp.length === 0 ? got.length === 0 : exp.some((e) => got.some((g) => g.includes(e) || e.includes(g)));

for (const model of models) {
  const extract = openAiExtractor({ OPENAI_API_KEY: process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY, EXTRACTOR_MODEL: model });
  let ok = 0; const ms: number[] = []; const misses: string[] = [];
  for (const c of cases) {
    const r = await extract(c.q, "2026-10-09");
    if (!r.ok) { misses.push(`${c.q}: ${r.reason} ${r.detail ?? ""}`); continue; }
    ms.push(r.ms);
    const e = c.entities!; const g = r.entities;
    const good = overlaps(norm(g.drugs), norm(e.drugs)) && overlaps(norm(g.conditions), norm(e.conditions)) && overlaps(norm(g.sponsors), norm(e.sponsors)) && overlaps(norm(g.countries), norm(e.countries))
      && (e.start_year ?? null) === g.start_year && (e.end_year ?? null) === g.end_year && (e.limit ?? null) === g.limit;
    if (good) ok++; else misses.push(`${c.q}: got ${JSON.stringify(g)}`);
  }
  ms.sort((a, b) => a - b);
  console.log(`\n${model}: ${ok}/${cases.length} correct · p50 ${ms[Math.floor(ms.length / 2)] ?? 0} ms · p90 ${ms[Math.floor(ms.length * 0.9)] ?? 0} ms`);
  for (const m of misses) console.log(`  - ${m}`);
}
```

Run: `npm run eval:extract`. Expected: both models above 20/24. Pick the faster model that is at least as correct, set `EXTRACTOR_MODEL` and `TITLER_MODEL` in `packages/engine/src/openai.ts` to it, and paste the two result lines into `eval/report.md` under a heading `## Extractor benchmark`. If a model rejects `reasoning.effort = "minimal"` (HTTP 400 naming the parameter), change `REASONING_EFFORT` to `"low"` and re-run.

### Task 22: Example runs

**Files:**
- Create: `examples/run.ts`, `examples/README.md` (generated), `examples/*.json` (generated)

- [ ] **Step 1: The script**

`examples/run.ts`:

```ts
import { mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { QueryRequest, type QueryRequestInput } from "@cheiron/contracts";
import { runQuery } from "@cheiron/engine";
import { depsFromEnv } from "../apps/api/src/deps";

const EXAMPLES: { slug: string; request: QueryRequestInput }[] = [
  { slug: "time-trend-pembrolizumab", request: { query: "How has the number of trials for this drug changed per year since 2015?", drug_name: "Pembrolizumab" } },
  { slug: "distribution-melanoma-phases", request: { query: "How are melanoma trials distributed across phases?" } },
  { slug: "comparison-pembrolizumab-nivolumab", request: { query: "Compare phases for trials involving pembrolizumab vs nivolumab" } },
  { slug: "geographic-lupus-recruiting", request: { query: "Which countries have the most recruiting trials for lupus?" } },
  { slug: "network-sponsors-drugs-melanoma", request: { query: "Show a network of sponsors and drugs for melanoma trials", citations_per_datum: 3 } },
];

const deps = depsFromEnv();
const dir = new URL("./", import.meta.url);
mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
const index: string[] = ["# Example runs", "", "Produced by `npm run examples` against the live APIs. Each file holds the request and the full response.", "", "| File | Question | Type | Total trials | Jev | Total |", "|---|---|---|---|---|---|"];
for (const ex of EXAMPLES) {
  const request = QueryRequest.parse(ex.request);
  const r = await runQuery(request, deps);
  writeFileSync(new URL(`./${ex.slug}.json`, dir), JSON.stringify({ request: ex.request, status: r.status, response: r.body }, null, 2));
  if (r.ok) index.push(`| [${ex.slug}.json](./${ex.slug}.json) | ${request.query} | ${r.body.visualization?.type} | ${r.body.meta.coverage.total_count.toLocaleString("en-US")} | ${r.body.meta.timing.jev_ms} ms | ${r.body.meta.timing.total_ms} ms |`);
  else index.push(`| [${ex.slug}.json](./${ex.slug}.json) | ${request.query} | ERROR ${r.status} | | | |`);
  console.log(ex.slug, r.status, r.ok ? r.body.visualization?.type : JSON.stringify(r.body));
}
writeFileSync(new URL("./README.md", dir), index.join("\n") + "\n");
```

Write `README.md` with these sections, in this order, with this content. Prose, short sentences, tables where the spec uses tables. Pull wording from `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-cheiron-design.md` and numbers from `eval/report.md` and `examples/README.md`; do not invent numbers.

1. **Cheiron** – one paragraph: what it does, the one-sentence approach (judged pipeline), link to the spec.
2. **Run it** – prerequisites (Node ≥ 22), `cp .env.example .env` and the two keys, `npm install`, `npm run dev` (API on 3001, web on 5173), `npm run build && npm start` (one process on 3001 serving both), `npm run check`, `npm run eval:jev`, `npm run examples`, Docker build and run.
3. **API** – `POST /query`: the request table from the spec (every field, type, required, validation); the response contract: the `visualization` union with one subsection per type listing `encoding` fields and the row shape, the row conventions from Task 13; `meta` field by field; the error table; `POST /query/stream` events; `GET /schema`, `GET /health`. Include one full example request and a trimmed example response copied from `examples/distribution-melanoma-phases.json`.
4. **How it decides** – the pipeline diagram (ASCII from the spec); the eleven Jev questions as a table (key, type, options); the thresholds table; the chart compatibility matrix; what Jev cannot do (free text, counting, arithmetic) and what code does instead; why there is no fallback from Jev to a generative classifier.
5. **Data handling** – the 19 fields; identity sets (MeSH first); multi-valued counting; truncation and `StartDate:desc`; caching; the exact Essie expressions used.
6. **Citations** – what a citation is, how the excerpt is chosen per dimension, what an edge cites, `meta.source.requests`.
7. **Example runs** – the table from `examples/README.md` with a sentence on each.
8. **Validation** – the Jev agreement table and latency from `eval/report.md`; the extractor benchmark lines; the test layout (what each package's tests pin, offline fixtures); the review-focus list from this plan as "cases pinned by tests".
9. **Design decisions and trade-offs** – the two rejected approaches and why; Jev plus extraction instead of one LLM call; download-and-aggregate instead of count queries; projected fields and the cap; LLM title grounded in the plan; zod as the single source of the contract.
10. **Limitations and next steps** – sequential paging (partition by StartDate ranges to parallelise), no synonym expansion for brand names, MeSH fallback inconsistency, no server-side persistence, English only, deployment.
11. **AI tools** – built with Claude Code (Claude Fable 5.1) as the implementer from a human-reviewed spec and plan. Designed deliberately: the judged-pipeline shape, the Jev question set and criteria, the thresholds, the compatibility matrix, the contract, the citation rules, the evaluation set. Generated and adapted: boilerplate, the Recharts and D3 components, the CSS, the tests' scaffolding. The Jev client is adapted from the author's own earlier client. State how correctness was validated (the eval, the fixtures, the examples checked by hand).
